## `statrs::function::gamma::gamma`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma.jsonl`: Gamma function gamma(x) for real x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = -30..180 step 0.25 (poles at 0, -1, ..., -30; overflow above about 171.62), plus +-10^k for k = -300..-10 step 10
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. gamma(x) grows like ((x + g - 1/2)/e)^(x - 1/2); rounding the base to a double is amplified by the exponent, up to 171 * 2^-53 (about 2e-14) at the overflow edge x = 171.6, and by the same amount through the reflection formula for negative x; 1e-13 allows that plus a few ulps of further arithmetic
- **Worst case:** x = 1.715e2 gives +inf (mpmath 9.4833675668248e307), rel. error inf

## `statrs::function::gamma::ln_gamma`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gammaln.jsonl`: Logarithm of the absolute value of the gamma function, log|gamma(x)|, for real x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the points of the gammaln grid where Gamma(x) > 0 or x is a pole, 877 of its points (statrs's ln_gamma is the logarithm of Gamma, not of |Gamma|). The full grid: x = -30..180 step 0.25 (poles at 0, -1, ..., -30) and +-10^k for k = -300..-10 step 10 (the gamma grid), plus 10^k for k = 0..300 step 10, x = 1e305, 2e305, 2.5e305, 2.6e305, 1e306, 1e307, 1e308 and the largest double (log Gamma overflows above about 2.55e305), 1 +- d and 2 +- d for d in {1e-5, 1e-10, 1e-15} (rounded to doubles), and x = -100.5, -1000.25, -10000.5, -1000000.5, -999999999999999.5
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. For x > 0 the value is well conditioned once x - 1 and x - 2 are formed exactly (Sterbenz's lemma near the zeros at 1 and 2, where careful implementations such as fdlibm and Boost expand in x - 1 and x - 2; taking log(gamma(x)) there instead loses everything), and Stirling's series for large x costs a few ulps. For x < 0 the reflection formula log(pi / |sin(pi x)|) - log Gamma(1 - x) subtracts two terms; rounding them costs (|term 1| + |term 2|) / |result| times 2^-53, at most 664 on this grid (x = -2.75, near the zero at -2.7477), so up to 7.4e-14 is unavoidable. 2e-13 allows 2.7x that and matches the quantile tolerances. SciPy's worst error is 1.8e-15 except at 8 of the 12 points next to 1 and 2, where it loses digits as log(gamma(x)) would: 1.5e-12 at 1 +- 1e-5, up to 8.0e-7 at 1 +- 1e-10 and 2 - 1e-10, and 29% at the double nearest 2 - 1e-15 (the series DLMF 5.7.3 confirms mpmath)
- **Worst case:** x = 1e0 gives -4.440892098500626e-16 (mpmath 0e0), rel. error 2.0e292

## `statrs::function::gamma::digamma`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/digamma.jsonl`: Digamma function psi(x) = gamma'(x) / gamma(x) for real x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = -30..180 step 0.25 (poles at 0, -1, ..., -30) and +-10^k for k = -300..-10 step 10 (the gamma grid), plus 10^k for k = 0..300 step 10, x = 1e305, 2e305, 2.5e305, 2.6e305, 1e306, 1e307, 1e308 and the largest double, x = -100.5, -1000.25, -10000.5, -1000000.5, -999999999999999.5, and next to the positive zero x0 = 1.46163...: the double nearest x0 and x0 +- d for d in {1e-5, 1e-10} (rounded to doubles)
- **Tolerance:** 4e-13 relative. For x > 0 the value is well conditioned except next to the zero x0 = 1.4616..., where careful implementations (Boost, SciPy) expand in x - x0 with x0 held to extra precision, so the exact input still gives a result to a few ulps; the grid has the double nearest x0 and x0 +- 1e-5, 1e-10 to test this. For x < 0 the reflection formula psi(1 - x) - pi cot(pi x) subtracts two terms; rounding them costs (|term 1| + |term 2|) / |result| times 2^-53, at most 1313 on this grid (x = -22.75, near a zero at -22.7502), so up to 1.5e-13 is unavoidable. 4e-13 allows 2.7x that, as for gammaln. SciPy's worst error is 2.8e-13, at that point; elsewhere 2.2e-14
- **Worst case:** x = 1.4616321449683622e0 gives -1.406535478365445e-15 (mpmath -9.241265521729427e-17), rel. error 1.4e1

## `statrs::function::gamma::gamma_lr`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gammainc.jsonl`: Regularized lower incomplete gamma function P(a, x) = gamma(a, x) / Gamma(a)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000} x x in {0, 0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000, 2000}
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. P is the prefix x^a e^-x / Gamma(a + 1) times a well-conditioned series or continued fraction. On this grid the logarithms of the prefix's factors reach 1.6e4 in magnitude while their sum stays above -745; forming them separately in double precision loses up to 1.6e4 * 2^-53 (about 2e-12), and combining them before rounding (Temme; Boost) loses far less. 1e-12 demands the careful approach and is 3x the worst SciPy error on this grid (3.5e-13 at a = 1000, x = 500)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e3, x = 0e0 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XInvalid (mpmath 0e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::function::gamma::gamma_lr`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gammainc_large.jsonl`: Regularized lower incomplete gamma function P(a, x) for large shapes a >= 1e8
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {1e8, 1e9, 1e10, 1e15, 1e20} x x in {1e8, 2e8, a/2, 0.9a, 1.1a, 2a, 1e300}, plus x = a + k sqrt(a) for a in {1e8, 1e9} and k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 0, 1, 3, 10, 30}
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. The function and method of gammainc and gamma_cdf, with their tolerance. The condition number |x f(x) / P| grows like k sqrt(a) at x = a + k sqrt(a) (9.5e5 at a = 1e9, k = -30), so a method that rounds x / a would lose about 1e-10; one that forms x - a exactly (Sterbenz's lemma) and expands in (x - a) / a (Temme, DLMF 8.12; Boost) loses about k^2 2^-53 (1e-13 at k = 30). Far from the bulk P rounds to 0 or 1. SciPy's worst error is 2.7e-15 except in the lower tail at x = a - 10 sqrt(a) and a - 30 sqrt(a): 13% and 0.24% off at a = 1e8, 53% and 15% off at a = 1e9 (P about 7e-24 and 2e-198; numerical integration confirms mpmath)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e9, x = 9.99905131670195e8 gives 1.349518851668386e-3 (mpmath 1.3495243297381703e-3), rel. error 4.1e-6

## `statrs::function::beta::beta_reg`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/betainc.jsonl`: Regularized incomplete beta function I_x(a, b) = B(x; a, b) / B(a, b)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a, b in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 100, 1000, 10000} x x in {0.01, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 0.99}, plus x = a/(a+b)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. I is the prefix x^a (1-x)^b / (a B(a, b)) times a well-conditioned series or continued fraction. On this grid the logarithms of the prefix's factors reach 2.8e4 in magnitude (a = b = 1e4) while their sum stays above -745; forming them separately in double precision loses up to 2.8e4 * 2^-53 (about 3e-12), and combining them before rounding (Boost's Lanczos-based prefix) loses far less. 1e-12 demands the careful approach and is 6x the worst SciPy error on this grid (1.6e-13)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e4, b = 1e4, x = 5e-1 gives 5.000000000286743e-1 (mpmath 5e-1), rel. error 5.7e-11

## `statrs::function::beta::beta_reg`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/betainc_large.jsonl`: Regularized incomplete beta function I_x(a, b) for large a and b (same function as betainc)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a, b in {3001, 5000, 2e4, 1e5} x x = a/(a+b) + k sd, k in {-10, -5, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 5, 10}, sd the standard deviation of Beta(a, b)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Same reasoning as betainc: the logarithms of the prefix's factors reach 2.8e5 in magnitude here (a = b = 1e5), so forming them separately in double precision loses up to 2.8e5 * 2^-53 (about 3e-11), while combining them before rounding does not. 1e-12 is 7x the worst SciPy error on this grid (1.5e-13)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e5, b = 1e5, x = 5e-1 gives 4.9999969504052444e-1 (mpmath 5e-1), rel. error 6.1e-7

## `statrs::function::beta::beta_reg`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/betainc_switch.jsonl`: Regularized incomplete beta function I_x(a, b) where the choice between I_x(a, b) and 1 - I_(1-x)(b, a) matters (same function as betainc)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {1, 10, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5} x b in {0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.2, 0.5} x x = 1 - y, y in {1e-1, 1e-2, 1e-4, 1e-6, 1e-8, 1e-10}; a, b in {10, 100, 1000, 2900} with x halfway between (a+1)/(a+2b) and (a+1)/(a+b+2); 5 points from a random spot-check (finding 001); without a = 1e5 with y = 1e-4 and points in betainc
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Same reasoning as betainc: the logarithms of the prefix's factors reach 1.1e6 in magnitude here (ln Gamma(1e5)), so forming them separately in double precision loses up to about 1e-10, and computing a small I as 1 - I_(1-x)(b, a) magnifies that by (1 - I)/I. Combining them before rounding, and computing the smaller of I and 1 - I directly, avoids both. 1e-12 is 17x the worst SciPy error on this grid (5.9e-14)
- **Worst case:** a = 9.76989e4, b = 1.66e-2, x = 9.999973151e-1 gives 1.6739805489330628e-2 (mpmath 1.6739805729174086e-2), rel. error 1.4e-8

## `statrs::function::erf::erfc`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/erfc.jsonl`: Complementary error function erfc(x) = 1 - erf(x)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = -10..30 step 0.25, plus +-10^k for k = -300..300 step 10; results are subnormal from about x = 26.6 and zero beyond about 27.3
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. For positive x the value is about exp(-x^2)/(x sqrt(pi)), and merely rounding x^2 to a double costs up to x^2 * 2^-53 relative error (about 8e-14 at x = 26.5, where the result becomes subnormal); 1e-13 accepts that unavoidable loss and nothing more
- **Worst case:** x = 5e-1 gives 4.795001222363462e-1 (mpmath 4.795001221869535e-1), rel. error 1.0e-10

## `statrs::distribution::Normal::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/norm_cdf.jsonl`: Standard normal cumulative distribution function Phi(x) (mean 0, sd 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = -40..40 step 0.25, plus +-10^k for k = -300..300 step 10; Phi is subnormal from about x = -37.52, 0 below about -38.49 and rounds to 1 above about 8.3
- **Tolerance:** 4e-13 relative. Rounding a quantity proportional to x (such as x / sqrt(2)) to a double changes Phi by the condition number |x phi(x) / Phi(x)| times 2^-53. In the lower tail the condition number is about x^2 and reaches 1408 at the last normal result (x = -37.5), so up to 1.6e-13 is unavoidable for a method that rounds such a quantity; 4e-13 allows 2x that, as for iv_large. SciPy's worst normal result here is 1.1e-13 (x = -32.25); it flushes subnormal results at x = -37.75 and -38 to 0. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** x = -7.5e-1 gives 2.2662735239943582e-1 (mpmath 2.266273523768682e-1), rel. error 1.0e-10

## `statrs::distribution::Normal::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/norm_sf.jsonl`: Standard normal survival function Q(x) = 1 - Phi(x) = Phi(-x) (mean 0, sd 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = -40..40 step 0.25, plus +-10^k for k = -300..300 step 10 (the norm_cdf grid); Q is subnormal from about x = 37.52, 0 above about 38.49 and rounds to 1 below about -8.3
- **Tolerance:** 4e-13 relative. Q(x) = Phi(-x), so the reasoning of norm_cdf applies mirrored: the condition number |x phi(x) / Q(x)| is about x^2 in the upper tail and reaches 1408 at x = 37.5, so up to 1.6e-13 is unavoidable; 4e-13 allows 2x that. SciPy's worst normal result here is 1.1e-13 (x = 32.25)
- **Worst case:** x = 7.5e-1 gives 2.2662735239943582e-1 (mpmath 2.266273523768682e-1), rel. error 1.0e-10

## `statrs::distribution::Normal::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/norm_ppf.jsonl`: Standard normal quantile function Phi^-1(p) (mean 0, sd 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** p = 10^-k for k = 1..20 and 30..300 step 10, the doubles nearest 1 - 10^-k for k = 1..16, i/64 for i = 0..64, 1/2 +- 10^-k for k = 5, 10, 15, and 5e-324, 1e-310, 2^-1022; p = 0 and p = 1 are tagged pole (the quantile is -inf and +inf)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. The input p is an exact double and the quantities a quantile method needs, p - 1/2 (exact for 1/4 <= p <= 1 by Sterbenz's lemma), 1 - p (exact for p >= 1/2) and log p, are exact or correctly rounded, so nothing beyond a few ulps of arithmetic is unavoidable (Wichura's AS 241 reaches about 1e-16). The quantile is ill-conditioned in p near p = 1/2 (|p / (x phi(x))| grows without bound) but that only matters for an inexact p. 1e-13 as for the other functions; SciPy's worst on this grid is 2.1e-16
- **Worst case:** p = 9.53125e-1 gives 1.6759397227734443e0 (mpmath 1.6759397227734438e0), rel. error 2.6e-16

## `statrs::distribution::StudentsT::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/t_cdf.jsonl`: Student's t cumulative distribution function F(x; df) (location 0, scale 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** df in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 7.5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e8} x x = 0 and +-{1e-300, 1e-10, 0.01, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7.5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 100, 1e3, 1e5, 1e10, 1e20, 1e50, 1e100, 1e300}; the lower tail decays like |x|^-df, so it is 0 as a double for large |x| unless df is small
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. Rounding a quantity proportional to x or x^2 (such as x^2 / df) to a double changes F by the condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| times 2^-53. It is about x^2 where t is close to normal and tends to df in the power-law tail; its largest value at a normal result on this grid is 901 (df = 1e8, x = -30), so up to 1.0e-13 is unavoidable for a method that rounds such a quantity; 2e-13 allows about 2x that, as for norm_cdf. SciPy's worst error here is 1.03e-13 (df = 1e4, x = -30), except that it returns 0.5 at x = +-1e-10 for df = 1 (error 6.4e-11) and 0 at x = -1e300 for df <= 1. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** df = 1e0, x = -1e300 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.1830988618379065e-301), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::distribution::StudentsT::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/t_sf.jsonl`: Student's t survival function 1 - F(x; df) = F(-x; df) (location 0, scale 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the t_cdf grid (df in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 7.5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e8} x 53 values of x)
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. 1 - F(x) = F(-x) and the grid is symmetric, so the reasoning of t_cdf applies mirrored: the condition number |x f(x) / (1 - F(x))| reaches 901 at a normal result (df = 1e8, x = 30), so up to 1.0e-13 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows about 2x that. SciPy's worst error here is 1.03e-13 (df = 1e4, x = 30), except that it returns 0.5 at x = +-1e-10 for df = 1 and 0 at x = 1e300 for df <= 1
- **Worst case:** df = 1e0, x = 1e300 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.1830988618379065e-301), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::distribution::Gamma::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_cdf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution cumulative distribution function P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x x in {0, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300}, plus x = a + k sqrt(a) (rounded to a double) for k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. With rate 1 this is P(a, x), the function of the gammainc dataset, so it gets gammainc's tolerance (accepted by the owner, to be revisited). The condition number |x f(x) / P| reaches 3.0e4 (a = 1e6, x = a - 30 sqrt(a)), so a method that rounds x / a loses up to 3.3e-12 there; one that forms x - a exactly (Sterbenz's lemma, a/2 <= x <= 2a) and expands in (x - a) / a, as Temme's uniform expansion does (DLMF 8.12; Boost), loses only about k^2 2^-53 at x = a + k sqrt(a) (1e-13 at k = 30), so 1e-12 demands such a method. SciPy's worst error here is 3.5e-13 (a = 1000, x = 500), except 2.1e-10 at a = 1e6, x = 990000 (P = 5.4e-24; the finite sum DLMF 8.4.10 confirms mpmath) and a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 50, x = 1e-5. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** a = 5e1, x = 1e-5 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.28791718e-315), rel. error 1.5e-7

## `statrs::distribution::Gamma::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_sf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution survival function Q(a, x) = 1 - P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the gamma_cdf grid
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Q = 1 - P, with gamma_cdf's tolerance for the same reasons: the condition number |x f(x) / Q| reaches 3.0e4 at a = 1e6, x = a + 30 sqrt(a), and is about x in the upper tail (up to about 745 at a normal result). SciPy's worst error here is 7.4e-13 (a = 1000, x = a + 30 sqrt(a)), except a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 5, x = 750
- **Worst case:** a = 5e0, x = 7.5e2 gives 0e0 (mpmath 2.52052905e-316), rel. error 1.1e-8

## `statrs::distribution::ChiSquared::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_cdf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution cumulative distribution function P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x x in {0, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300}, plus x = a + k sqrt(a) (rounded to a double) for k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. With rate 1 this is P(a, x), the function of the gammainc dataset, so it gets gammainc's tolerance (accepted by the owner, to be revisited). The condition number |x f(x) / P| reaches 3.0e4 (a = 1e6, x = a - 30 sqrt(a)), so a method that rounds x / a loses up to 3.3e-12 there; one that forms x - a exactly (Sterbenz's lemma, a/2 <= x <= 2a) and expands in (x - a) / a, as Temme's uniform expansion does (DLMF 8.12; Boost), loses only about k^2 2^-53 at x = a + k sqrt(a) (1e-13 at k = 30), so 1e-12 demands such a method. SciPy's worst error here is 3.5e-13 (a = 1000, x = 500), except 2.1e-10 at a = 1e6, x = 990000 (P = 5.4e-24; the finite sum DLMF 8.4.10 confirms mpmath) and a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 50, x = 1e-5. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** a = 5e1, x = 1e-5 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.28791718e-315), rel. error 1.5e-7

## `statrs::distribution::ChiSquared::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_sf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution survival function Q(a, x) = 1 - P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the gamma_cdf grid
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Q = 1 - P, with gamma_cdf's tolerance for the same reasons: the condition number |x f(x) / Q| reaches 3.0e4 at a = 1e6, x = a + 30 sqrt(a), and is about x in the upper tail (up to about 745 at a normal result). SciPy's worst error here is 7.4e-13 (a = 1000, x = a + 30 sqrt(a)), except a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 5, x = 750
- **Worst case:** a = 5e0, x = 7.5e2 gives 0e0 (mpmath 2.52052905e-316), rel. error 1.1e-8

## `statrs::distribution::Gamma::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_ppf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution quantile function, the x with P(a, x) = p (shape a, rate 1); half the chi-squared quantile with df = 2a
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf); for small a the lower-tail quantiles are below the smallest double
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. p and a are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the lower tail the quantile is x = exp(L) with L = (log p + log Gamma(a + 1)) / a approximately; rounding L (or 1/a, which multiplies it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a nonzero, normal quantile on this grid is 691.3 (a = 0.01, p = 1e-3, x = 5.7e-301), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches t_ppf. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.1e-13 (a = 0.01, p = 0.01); it fails 6 points: 4.2e-12 to 4.7e-6 off at p = 5e-324 for a >= 100, and 4.7e-11 off at a = 1e6, p = 1e-10
- **Worst case:** a = 1e0, p = 5e-324 gives 9.313225746154785e-10 (mpmath 5e-324), rel. error 4.2e298

## `statrs::distribution::ChiSquared::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_ppf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution quantile function, the x with P(a, x) = p (shape a, rate 1); half the chi-squared quantile with df = 2a
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf); for small a the lower-tail quantiles are below the smallest double
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. p and a are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the lower tail the quantile is x = exp(L) with L = (log p + log Gamma(a + 1)) / a approximately; rounding L (or 1/a, which multiplies it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a nonzero, normal quantile on this grid is 691.3 (a = 0.01, p = 1e-3, x = 5.7e-301), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches t_ppf. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.1e-13 (a = 0.01, p = 0.01); it fails 6 points: 4.2e-12 to 4.7e-6 off at p = 5e-324 for a >= 100, and 4.7e-11 off at a = 1e6, p = 1e-10
- **Worst case:** a = 1e0, p = 5e-324 gives 4.656612873077393e-10 (mpmath 5e-324), rel. error 2.1e298

## `statrs::distribution::InverseGamma::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/invgamma_cdf.jsonl`: Inverse gamma distribution cumulative distribution function Q(a, 1/x) (shape a, rate 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 150, 1e3, 1e4} x x = 1/t (rounded to a double) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300} and t = a + k sqrt(a) (rounded to a double first) for k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive, plus x = 0, 5e-324 and 1e-310 (where 1/x overflows a double)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. F(x) = Q(a, 1/x) and 1 - F(x) = P(a, 1/x), the functions of gamma_sf and gamma_cdf, so they get gamma's tolerance 1e-12, and the condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| is gamma's at t = 1/x. Unlike gamma's x, t = 1/x is not an exact double: rounding it (as SciPy, Boost and statrs all do) costs the condition number times 2^-53. Shapes stop at 1e4, where the largest condition number at a normal result is 3.0e3 (t = a -+ 30 sqrt(a)) and rounding costs at most 3.4e-13; at gamma's a = 1e5 and 1e6 it would cost 1.1e-12 and 3.3e-12, beyond the tolerance, so a correct method that rounds 1/x would fail there. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions). SciPy's worst error here is 7.5e-13 (cdf, a = 1e3, x = 1/(a + 30 sqrt(a))), except one subnormal result flushed to 0 in each function (cdf at a = 5, x = 1/750; sf at a = 50, x = 1/1e-5)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e4, x = 1e-310 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XInvalid (mpmath 0e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::InverseGamma::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/invgamma_sf.jsonl`: Inverse gamma distribution survival function P(a, 1/x) = 1 - F(x) (shape a, rate 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the invgamma_cdf grid: a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 150, 1e3, 1e4} x x = 1/t (rounded to a double) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300} and t = a + k sqrt(a) (rounded to a double first) for k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive, plus x = 0, 5e-324 and 1e-310 (where 1/x overflows a double)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. F(x) = Q(a, 1/x) and 1 - F(x) = P(a, 1/x), the functions of gamma_sf and gamma_cdf, so they get gamma's tolerance 1e-12, and the condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| is gamma's at t = 1/x. Unlike gamma's x, t = 1/x is not an exact double: rounding it (as SciPy, Boost and statrs all do) costs the condition number times 2^-53. Shapes stop at 1e4, where the largest condition number at a normal result is 3.0e3 (t = a -+ 30 sqrt(a)) and rounding costs at most 3.4e-13; at gamma's a = 1e5 and 1e6 it would cost 1.1e-12 and 3.3e-12, beyond the tolerance, so a correct method that rounds 1/x would fail there. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions). SciPy's worst error here is 7.5e-13 (cdf, a = 1e3, x = 1/(a + 30 sqrt(a))), except one subnormal result flushed to 0 in each function (cdf at a = 5, x = 1/750; sf at a = 50, x = 1/1e-5)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e4, x = 1e-310 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XInvalid (mpmath 1e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::InverseGamma::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/invgamma_ppf.jsonl`: Inverse gamma distribution quantile function, the x with Q(a, 1/x) = p (shape a, rate 1); the reciprocal of the gamma quantile at 1 - p
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 150, 1e3, 1e4} x the gamma_ppf probabilities p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf); for small a the upper quantiles are above the largest double
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. As for gamma_ppf: the quantile is x = 1/t with t = exp(L), and rounding L (or 1/a, which multiplies it in the gamma lower tail) to a double changes x by |log x| times 2^-53. The largest |log x| at a finite, normal quantile on this grid is 691.3 (a = 0.01, p = 0.999, x = 1.8e300), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches gamma_ppf. Quantiles above the largest double are tagged overflow. SciPy fails 11 of the 15 points with p = 5e-324 (5.5e-13 to 1.6e-4 off; it is right at a = 1, 1.5, 2 and 3) and no other point
- **Worst case:** a = 1.5e2, p = 1e-10 gives 5.859375e-3 (mpmath 4.14064600442094e-3), rel. error 4.2e-1

## `statrs::distribution::Chi::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/chi_cdf.jsonl`: Chi distribution cumulative distribution function P(k/2, x^2/2) (k degrees of freedom)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** k in {1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 160, 161, 300, 1e3, 1e4} x x = sqrt(2 t) (rounded to a double) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300} and t = a + j sqrt(a) with a = k/2 (rounded to a double first) for j in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive, plus x = 0, 5e-324, 1e-170, 4e-162 and 1e-160 (where x * x / 2 underflows to 0, 1e-323 or a subnormal), 1e155 and 1e300 (where x * x overflows)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. F(x) = P(k/2, x^2/2) and 1 - F(x) = Q(k/2, x^2/2), the functions of gamma_cdf and gamma_sf, so they get gamma's tolerance 1e-12. The condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| is twice gamma's at t = x^2/2, but t is not an exact double: rounding x * x (as SciPy and statrs do; halving is exact) costs gamma's condition number at t times 2^-53. Degrees of freedom stop at 1e4 (shape 5e3), where gamma's largest condition number at a normal result is 2.1e3 (t = a -+ 30 sqrt(a)) and rounding costs at most 2.4e-13; at k = 2e6 (gamma's a = 1e6) it would cost 3.3e-12, beyond the tolerance. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions). SciPy's worst error at the other points is 3.0e-13 (sf, k = 1000, x = sqrt(2 (a + 3 sqrt(a)))); it is 1.3e-12 and 5.1e-12 off at k = 1e4 (cdf at x = 75.88, sf at x = 119.34, where the finite sum DLMF 8.4.10 confirms mpmath), flushes 1 cdf and 3 sf subnormal results to 0, and for k = 1 loses digits where x * x is subnormal (x = 4e-162 and 1e-160) and returns 0 where it underflows (x = 1e-170)
- **Worst case:** k = 1e4, x = 1e300 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XInvalid (mpmath 1e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::Chi::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/chi_sf.jsonl`: Chi distribution survival function Q(k/2, x^2/2) = 1 - F(x) (k degrees of freedom)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the chi_cdf grid: k in {1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 160, 161, 300, 1e3, 1e4} x x = sqrt(2 t) (rounded to a double) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300} and t = a + j sqrt(a) with a = k/2 (rounded to a double first) for j in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive, plus x = 0, 5e-324, 1e-170, 4e-162 and 1e-160 (where x * x / 2 underflows to 0, 1e-323 or a subnormal), 1e155 and 1e300 (where x * x overflows)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. F(x) = P(k/2, x^2/2) and 1 - F(x) = Q(k/2, x^2/2), the functions of gamma_cdf and gamma_sf, so they get gamma's tolerance 1e-12. The condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| is twice gamma's at t = x^2/2, but t is not an exact double: rounding x * x (as SciPy and statrs do; halving is exact) costs gamma's condition number at t times 2^-53. Degrees of freedom stop at 1e4 (shape 5e3), where gamma's largest condition number at a normal result is 2.1e3 (t = a -+ 30 sqrt(a)) and rounding costs at most 2.4e-13; at k = 2e6 (gamma's a = 1e6) it would cost 3.3e-12, beyond the tolerance. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions). SciPy's worst error at the other points is 3.0e-13 (sf, k = 1000, x = sqrt(2 (a + 3 sqrt(a)))); it is 1.3e-12 and 5.1e-12 off at k = 1e4 (cdf at x = 75.88, sf at x = 119.34, where the finite sum DLMF 8.4.10 confirms mpmath), flushes 1 cdf and 3 sf subnormal results to 0, and for k = 1 loses digits where x * x is subnormal (x = 4e-162 and 1e-160) and returns 0 where it underflows (x = 1e-170)
- **Worst case:** k = 1e4, x = 1e300 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XInvalid (mpmath 0e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::Chi::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/chi_ppf.jsonl`: Chi distribution quantile function, the x with P(k/2, x^2/2) = p (k degrees of freedom); sqrt(2 t) with t the gamma quantile
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** k in {1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 160, 161, 300, 1e3, 1e4} x the gamma_ppf probabilities p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf)
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. As for gamma_ppf: the quantile is x = sqrt(2 t) with t = exp(L), and rounding L (or 2/k, which multiplies it in the lower tail) to a double changes t by |L| times 2^-53 and x by half that. The largest |L| / 2 = |log(x / sqrt(2))| at a nonzero, normal quantile on this grid is 690.9 (k = 1, p = 1e-300, x = 1.25e-300), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches gamma_ppf. Subnormal quantiles are judged relative to 2.2e-308 (conventions). SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.8e-14 (k = 3, p = 1e-300); it fails 4: 0 instead of 1.25e-300 at k = 1, p = 1e-300, and 1.2e-9 to 1.6e-8 off at p = 5e-324 for k >= 300
- **Worst case:** k = 1e0, p = 5e-324 gives 6.668276248455232e-162 (mpmath 5e-324), rel. error 3.0e146

## `statrs::distribution::Weibull::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/weibull_cdf.jsonl`: Weibull distribution cumulative distribution function 1 - exp(-(x/lambda)^k) (shape k, scale lambda)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** shape k in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.1, 3.5, 10, 100} x scale lambda = 2^m for m in {-500, -10, 0, 10, 500} x x = u lambda with u the double nearest t^(1/k) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 37, 50, 100, 300, 700, 708.5, 740, 745.5, 1e3, 1e10, 1e300}, kept where u and x are normal and finite (so x/lambda = u exactly), plus x = 0 and x = -lambda; F is 0 for x <= 0 and rounds to 1 above about t = 37.4
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. x, k and lambda are exact doubles and lambda is a power of two, so x/lambda is exact and only t = (x/lambda)^k must be rounded (pow is within an ulp or so in any good libm), then -expm1(-t) is within an ulp. The condition number in t, t / (e^t - 1), is at most 1, so a careful method loses a few ulps at most. 1e-13 as for the other functions; results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** k = 1e2, lambda = 3.273390607896142e150, x = 3.273390607896142e153 gives NaN (mpmath 1e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::Weibull::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/weibull_sf.jsonl`: Weibull distribution survival function exp(-(x/lambda)^k) (shape k, scale lambda)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the weibull_cdf grid: shape k in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.1, 3.5, 10, 100} x scale lambda = 2^m for m in {-500, -10, 0, 10, 500} x x = u lambda with u the double nearest t^(1/k) for t in {1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 37, 50, 100, 300, 700, 708.5, 740, 745.5, 1e3, 1e10, 1e300}, kept where u and x are normal and finite (so x/lambda = u exactly), plus x = 0 and x = -lambda; Q is 1 for x <= 0, subnormal from about t = 708.40 and 0 above about t = 745.13
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. x, k and lambda are exact doubles and lambda is a power of two, so x/lambda is exact and only t = (x/lambda)^k must be rounded. A good libm's pow is within about half an ulp (glibc's bound is 0.52 ulp, 1.04 2^-53 relative). The condition number in t is t itself, 708.4 at the last normal result, so that rounding costs up to 8.2e-14 there (7.9e-14 if correctly rounded, as for expon_sf with another rate); a pow error of a full ulp would cost 1.6e-13. SciPy's worst error here is 5.4e-14. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** k = 1e2, lambda = 3.273390607896142e150, x = 3.273390607896142e153 gives NaN (mpmath 0e0), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::Weibull::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/weibull_ppf.jsonl`: Weibull distribution quantile function lambda (-log(1 - p))^(1/k)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** shape k in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.1, 3.5, 10, 100} x scale lambda = 2^m for m in {-500, -10, 0, 10, 500} x the gamma_ppf probabilities: p = 0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15 and 1; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. p is an exact double, so L = -log1p(-p) is within an ulp or so; raising it to 1/k multiplies that error by 1/k (at most 10 on this grid) and adds an ulp, and multiplying by lambda (a power of two) is exact. 1/k itself is not a double for most k: rounding it (or log(L) / k) costs |log(x/lambda)| 2^-53, at most 744.4 2^-53 = 8.3e-14 at a normal result on this grid (p = 5e-324). 1e-13 as for the other functions; results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** k = 1e2, lambda = 3.273390607896142e150, p = 9.99999999999999e-1 gives +inf (mpmath 3.3914155100705836e150), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::LogNormal::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/lognorm_cdf.jsonl`: Log-normal distribution cumulative distribution function Phi((ln x - mu) / sigma) (location mu, scale sigma)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** (mu, sigma) in {(0, 0.25), (-3, 0.5), (0, 1), (2, 4)} x x = the double nearest exp(mu + sigma w) for w = -40..40 step 0.5, plus x = 0 and x = -1; F is 0 for x <= 0, subnormal from about w = -37.52, 0 below about w = -38.49 and rounds to 1 above about w = 8.3
- **Tolerance:** 8e-13 relative. x is an exact double but ln x is not: rounding ln x costs |ln x| 2^-53 in w = (ln x - mu) / sigma after dividing by sigma, and rounding w costs |w| 2^-53. Phi's condition number in w, phi(w) / Phi(w), is about |w| in the lower tail, so a method that rounds ln x and w loses (|ln x| / sigma + |w|) phi(w) / Phi(w) 2^-53, at most 3.4e-13 at a normal result on this grid (mu = -3, sigma = 0.5, w = -37.5); 8e-13 is 2.35x that, the margin norm_cdf keeps (4e-13 over its 1.6e-13: 2.5x) for roundings of up to an ulp rather than half an ulp. SciPy flushes the subnormal result at w = -38 to 0. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** mu = 2e0, sigma = 4e0, x = 1.353352832366127e-1 gives 1.5865525394505725e-1 (mpmath 1.5865525393145705e-1), rel. error 8.6e-11

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## `statrs::distribution::LogNormal::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/lognorm_sf.jsonl`: Log-normal distribution survival function Phi(-(ln x - mu) / sigma) (location mu, scale sigma)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the lognorm_cdf grid: (mu, sigma) in {(0, 0.25), (-3, 0.5), (0, 1), (2, 4)} x x = the double nearest exp(mu + sigma w) for w = -40..40 step 0.5, plus x = 0 and x = -1; 1 - F is 1 for x <= 0, subnormal from about w = 37.52, 0 above about w = 38.49 and rounds to 1 below about w = -8.3
- **Tolerance:** 8e-13 relative. The reasoning of lognorm_cdf mirrored: rounding ln x and w = (ln x - mu) / sigma costs (|ln x| / sigma + |w|) phi(w) / Phi(-w) 2^-53, about |w| (|ln x| / sigma + |w|) 2^-53 in the upper tail, at most 3.2e-13 at a normal result on this grid (mu = 2, sigma = 4, w = 37.5); 8e-13 as for lognorm_cdf (2.5x). SciPy flushes the subnormal result at w = 38 to 0. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** mu = -3e0, sigma = 5e-1, x = 8.20849986238988e-2 gives 1.5865525394505725e-1 (mpmath 1.5865525393145702e-1), rel. error 8.6e-11

## `statrs::distribution::LogNormal::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/lognorm_ppf.jsonl`: Log-normal distribution quantile function exp(mu + sigma Phi^-1(p))
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** (mu, sigma) in {(0, 0.25), (-3, 0.5), (0, 1), (2, 4)} x the norm_ppf probabilities: p = 10^-k for k = 1..20 and 30..300 step 10, the doubles nearest 1 - 10^-k for k = 1..16, i/64 for i = 0..64, 1/2 +- 10^-k for k = 5, 10, 15, and 5e-324, 1e-310, 2^-1022; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. The quantile is exp(y) with y = mu + sigma Phi^-1(p), so an absolute error in y is the relative error of the result. Rounding y costs |y| 2^-53, and a normal quantile q good to a few ulps (norm_ppf: within 2^-53 x 2.4 for statrs and SciPy) costs sigma |q| times that; |y| and sigma |q| are at most 152 and 154 on this grid (mu = 2, sigma = 4, p = 5e-324), so a careful method stays within about 6e-14. 1e-13 as for the other quantiles; results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** mu = 2e0, sigma = 4e0, p = 1e-300 gives 3.244822840874672e-64 (mpmath 3.244822840874781e-64), rel. error 3.3e-14

## `statrs::distribution::Exp::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/expon_cdf.jsonl`: Exponential distribution cumulative distribution function 1 - e^-x (rate 1); the gamma distribution with shape 1
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** x = 10^-k for k = 1..20, 5e-324, 1e-310, x = 0..40 step 0.25, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 705, 708, 708.25, 708.5, 709, 709.75, 710, 720, 730, 740, 744, 745, 745.125, 745.25, 746, 750, 1000, +-10^k for k = -300..300 step 10, -0.25 and -1; F is 0 for x <= 0 and rounds to 1 above about 37.4
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. x is an exact double and -expm1(-x) of an exact argument is within an ulp or so in any good libm, so nothing beyond a few ulps is unavoidable. The condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| = x / (e^x - 1) is at most 1, so even a method that rounds a quantity proportional to x (rate times x for another rate) loses at most 2^-53. 1e-13 as for the other functions; SciPy's worst error here is 2.2e-16 (one subnormal step at x = 5e-324 and 1e-310, judged relative to 2.2e-308)
- **Worst case:** x = 1e-17 gives 0e0 (mpmath 1e-17), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::distribution::Exp::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/expon_sf.jsonl`: Exponential distribution survival function e^-x (rate 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the expon_cdf grid: x = 10^-k for k = 1..20, 5e-324, 1e-310, x = 0..40 step 0.25, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 705, 708, 708.25, 708.5, 709, 709.75, 710, 720, 730, 740, 744, 745, 745.125, 745.25, 746, 750, 1000, +-10^k for k = -300..300 step 10, -0.25 and -1; Q is 1 for x <= 0, subnormal from about x = 708.40 and 0 above about 745.13
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. x is an exact double and exp(-x) of an exact argument is within an ulp or so in any good libm. The condition number |x f(x) / Q(x)| = x reaches 708.4 at the last normal result, so a method that rounds a quantity proportional to x (rate times x for another rate, not checked here) would lose up to 7.9e-14 there; with rate 1 nothing is rounded. 1e-13 as for the other functions; SciPy's worst error here is 2.1e-16. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** x = 1e300 gives 0e0 (mpmath 0e0), rel. error 0.0e0

## `statrs::distribution::Exp::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/expon_ppf.jsonl`: Exponential distribution quantile function -log(1 - p) (rate 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the norm_ppf grid: p = 10^-k for k = 1..20 and 30..300 step 10, the doubles nearest 1 - 10^-k for k = 1..16, i/64 for i = 0..64, 1/2 +- 10^-k for k = 5, 10, 15, and 5e-324, 1e-310, 2^-1022; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-13 relative. p is an exact double, so -p is exact and -log1p(-p) is within an ulp or so in any good libm (log(1 - p) also, for p >= 1/2, where 1 - p is exact); nothing beyond a few ulps is unavoidable. 1e-13 as for the other functions; SciPy's worst error here is 1.6e-16
- **Worst case:** p = 6.5625e-1 gives 1.0678406300013559e0 (mpmath 1.067840630001356e0), rel. error 2.1e-16

## `statrs::distribution::Erlang::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_cdf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution cumulative distribution function P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the whole-number shapes of the gamma_cdf grid (a = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6), 409 of its points. The full grid: a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x x in {0, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 700, 750, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e7, 1e10, 1e300}, plus x = a + k sqrt(a) (rounded to a double) for k in {-30, -10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10, 30} where positive
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. With rate 1 this is P(a, x), the function of the gammainc dataset, so it gets gammainc's tolerance (accepted by the owner, to be revisited). The condition number |x f(x) / P| reaches 3.0e4 (a = 1e6, x = a - 30 sqrt(a)), so a method that rounds x / a loses up to 3.3e-12 there; one that forms x - a exactly (Sterbenz's lemma, a/2 <= x <= 2a) and expands in (x - a) / a, as Temme's uniform expansion does (DLMF 8.12; Boost), loses only about k^2 2^-53 at x = a + k sqrt(a) (1e-13 at k = 30), so 1e-12 demands such a method. SciPy's worst error here is 3.5e-13 (a = 1000, x = 500), except 2.1e-10 at a = 1e6, x = 990000 (P = 5.4e-24; the finite sum DLMF 8.4.10 confirms mpmath) and a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 50, x = 1e-5. Results below 2.2e-308 are judged relative to that number (conventions)
- **Worst case:** a = 5e1, x = 1e-5 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.28791718e-315), rel. error 1.5e-7

## `statrs::distribution::Erlang::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_sf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution survival function Q(a, x) = 1 - P(a, x) (shape a, rate 1); chi-squared with df = 2a at 2x
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the whole-number shapes of the gamma_sf grid (a = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6), 409 of its points. The full grid: the gamma_cdf grid
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Q = 1 - P, with gamma_cdf's tolerance for the same reasons: the condition number |x f(x) / Q| reaches 3.0e4 at a = 1e6, x = a + 30 sqrt(a), and is about x in the upper tail (up to about 745 at a normal result). SciPy's worst error here is 7.4e-13 (a = 1000, x = a + 30 sqrt(a)), except a subnormal result flushed to 0 at a = 5, x = 750
- **Worst case:** a = 5e0, x = 7.5e2 gives 0e0 (mpmath 2.52052905e-316), rel. error 1.1e-8

## `statrs::distribution::Erlang::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/gamma_ppf.jsonl`: Gamma distribution quantile function, the x with P(a, x) = p (shape a, rate 1); half the chi-squared quantile with df = 2a
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the whole-number shapes of the gamma_ppf grid (a = 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6), 324 of its points. The full grid: a in {0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6} x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf); for small a the lower-tail quantiles are below the smallest double
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. p and a are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the lower tail the quantile is x = exp(L) with L = (log p + log Gamma(a + 1)) / a approximately; rounding L (or 1/a, which multiplies it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a nonzero, normal quantile on this grid is 691.3 (a = 0.01, p = 1e-3, x = 5.7e-301), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches t_ppf. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.1e-13 (a = 0.01, p = 0.01); it fails 6 points: 4.2e-12 to 4.7e-6 off at p = 5e-324 for a >= 100, and 4.7e-11 off at a = 1e6, p = 1e-10
- **Worst case:** a = 1e0, p = 5e-324 gives 9.313225746154785e-10 (mpmath 5e-324), rel. error 4.2e298

## `statrs::distribution::Beta::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/beta_cdf.jsonl`: Beta distribution cumulative distribution function I_x(a, b) on [0, 1], including both tails and the ends of the support
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a, b in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 100, 1000, 10000} x x in {0, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}, plus x = a/(a+b) + k sd (rounded to a double) for k in {-10, -3, -1, 0, 1, 3, 10} where 0 < x < 1, sd the standard deviation of Beta(a, b)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Same function as betainc, so betainc's tolerance (accepted by the owner, to be revisited). The prefix x^a (1-x)^b / B(a, b) of the smaller of I and 1 - I is a product of factors whose logarithms (a ln x, b ln(1 - x), ln Gamma(a), ln Gamma(b), ln Gamma(a + b)) reach 1.78e5 in magnitude at a normal result on this grid (ln Gamma(2e4) at a = b = 1e4); rounding each to a double separately loses up to 1.78e5 * 2^-53 = 2.0e-11, while combining them before rounding (Boost's Lanczos-based prefix) does not, so 1e-12 demands the careful method. SciPy's worst error here is 1.1e-13 (a = b = 1000, x = 0.3), except 1.1e-9 at a = b = 1/2, x = 1 - 1e-15, where the closed form (2/pi) asin(sqrt x) confirms mpmath. The ends of the support give 0 and 1 exactly
- **Worst case:** a = 1e4, b = 1e-1, x = 9.99999999999999e-1 gives 1e0 (mpmath 9.165122714031607e-1), rel. error 9.1e-2

#### Crate wrong (156 points, worst 20 shown)

## `statrs::distribution::Beta::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/beta_sf.jsonl`: Beta distribution survival function 1 - I_x(a, b) on [0, 1]
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the beta_cdf grid
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. 1 - I, with beta_cdf's tolerance for the same reasons (largest prefix logarithm 1.78e5 at a = b = 1e4). Computed as the complement 1 - I only where I is not close to 1, the upper tail keeps full relative precision, which this tolerance demands down to the smallest normal double. SciPy's worst error here is 1.0e-15, except at a = b = 1/2, x = 1e-20, where it returns 1 for 1 - (2/pi) 1e-10 (6.4e-11 off; the closed form confirms mpmath)
- **Worst case:** a = 1e-1, b = 1e4, x = 1.0486206385669913e-4 gives 2.2330569715089843e-2 (mpmath 2.2330569703971435e-2), rel. error 5.0e-10

## `statrs::distribution::FisherSnedecor::cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/f_cdf.jsonl`: F (Fisher-Snedecor) distribution cumulative distribution function F(x; d1, d2) = I_z(d1/2, d2/2) with z = d1 x / (d1 x + d2)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** d1, d2 in {0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 100, 1000, 1e5} (every pair) x x in {0, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 100, 1e3, 1e5, 1e10, 1e20, 1e100, 1e300}, plus x = 1 + k s (rounded to a double) for k in {-10, -3, -1, 1, 3, 10} where x > 0, s = sqrt(2/d1 + 2/d2) (about the standard deviation of log F)
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. Same function as betainc (F(x) = I_z(d1/2, d2/2)), so betainc's tolerance (accepted by the owner, to be revisited). Two error sources: (1) forming z = d1 x / (d1 x + d2) or y = 1 - z = d2 / (d1 x + d2) directly costs a few roundings, which change F by the condition number |x f(x) / F(x)| (= z^a y^b / (B(a, b) F)) times 2^-53 each; its largest value at a normal result on this grid is 1648 (d1 = d2 = 1e5, x = 0.937), so about 1.8e-13 per rounding. Forming y as 1 - z instead loses 2^-53 z / y relative to y, unbounded as x grows, and is not needed. (2) The prefix z^a y^b / B(a, b) has logarithms up to 1.05e6 (ln Gamma(1e5) at d1 = d2 = 1e5); rounding them separately costs up to 1.2e-10, combining them before rounding (Boost's Lanczos-based prefix) does not, so 1e-12 demands the careful method. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 9.9e-13 (d1 = 1e5, d2 = 10, x = 0.553); it fails 6: 1.1e-12 to 1.7e-12 at d1 = 1e5 with d2 = 10 or 30 (it forms z near 1 and betainc rounds 1 - z), and 1 instead of 1 - 6.4e-11 at d1 = d2 = 1, x = 1e20 (the closed form (2/pi) atan(sqrt x) confirms mpmath). x = 0 gives 0 exactly
- **Worst case:** d1 = 1e5, d2 = 5e-1, x = 1e10 gives 1e0 (mpmath 9.975330343401949e-1), rel. error 2.5e-3

## `statrs::distribution::FisherSnedecor::sf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/f_sf.jsonl`: F (Fisher-Snedecor) distribution survival function 1 - F(x; d1, d2)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** the f_cdf grid
- **Tolerance:** 1e-12 relative. 1 - F, with f_cdf's tolerance for the same reasons: the condition number |x f / (1 - F)| reaches 1549 at a normal result (d1 = d2 = 1e5, x = 1.063), about 1.7e-13 per rounding of z or y, and the prefix logarithms reach 1.05e6, so separately rounded logarithms can cost 1.2e-10. Computed from y = d2 / (d1 x + d2) directly, the upper tail keeps full relative precision for every x, which this tolerance demands down to the smallest normal double. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 8.6e-13 (d1 = 30, d2 = 1e5, x = 5); it fails 11: 1.0e-12 to 2.4e-12 at d2 = 1e5 with d1 = 10 or 30, and 1 instead of 1 - 6.4e-11 at d1 = d2 = 1, x = 1e-20 (the closed form confirms mpmath)
- **Worst case:** d1 = 1e5, d2 = 3e1, x = 1e20 gives 0e0 (mpmath 3.3556851419183707e-295), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::stats_tests::ttest_onesample::ttest_onesample`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/ttest_1samp.jsonl`: One-sample t-test: statistic t = (mean - mu) / (s / sqrt(n)) and its p-value from Student's t with n - 1 degrees of freedom; alternative -1 less, 0 two-sided, 1 greater
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** samples x_i = loc + ((7919 i) mod 1000 - 500) / 1000, i = 0..n-1, for n in {2, 3, 5, 10, 30, 100} and loc in {0, 10}; mu = loc + d for d in {-30, -3, -0.3, -0.01, 0.001, 0.1, 1, 10, 100}; each with alternative -1 (less), 0 (two-sided) and 1 (greater). |t| runs from 0.017 to 3410 and the p-value from 4.3e-253 to 1
- **Tolerance:** 5e-12 relative. For both outputs. The inputs are exact, but t is formed from sums of n rounded terms, so a careful method is off by up to about (kappa_t + n) 2^-53, kappa_t being t's relative condition number in the data and mu (ttest_condition); the p-value adds the factor kappa_p = |t f(t) / P| (at most 99 here). The largest bound max(kappa_p, 1) (kappa_t + n) 2^-53 on this grid is 4.5e-12 (n = 100, loc = 10, mu = 9.99, where t = 0.017 and kappa_t = 4.0e4: mean - mu cancels); 5e-12 is the smallest round value above it (tested). SciPy's worst error is 1.7e-12, at that point
- **Worst case:** x = 9.5e0, 1.0419e1, 1.0338e1, 1.0257e1, 1.0176e1, 1.0095e1, 1.0014e1, 9.933e0, 9.852e0, 9.771e0, 9.69e0, 9.609e0, 9.528e0, 1.0447e1, 1.0366e1, 1.0285e1, 1.0204e1, 1.0123e1, 1.0042e1, 9.961e0, 9.88e0, 9.799e0, 9.718e0, 9.637e0, 9.556e0, 1.0475e1, 1.0394e1, 1.0313e1, 1.0232e1, 1.0151e1, 1.007e1, 9.989e0, 9.908e0, 9.827e0, 9.746e0, 9.665e0, 9.584e0, 9.503e0, 1.0422e1, 1.0341e1, 1.026e1, 1.0179e1, 1.0098e1, 1.0017e1, 9.936e0, 9.855e0, 9.774e0, 9.693e0, 9.612e0, 9.531e0, 1.045e1, 1.0369e1, 1.0288e1, 1.0207e1, 1.0126e1, 1.0045e1, 9.964e0, 9.883e0, 9.802e0, 9.721e0, 9.64e0, 9.559e0, 1.0478e1, 1.0397e1, 1.0316e1, 1.0235e1, 1.0154e1, 1.0073e1, 9.992e0, 9.911e0, 9.83e0, 9.749e0, 9.668e0, 9.587e0, 9.506e0, 1.0425e1, 1.0344e1, 1.0263e1, 1.0182e1, 1.0101e1, 1.002e1, 9.939e0, 9.858e0, 9.777e0, 9.696e0, 9.615e0, 9.534e0, 1.0453e1, 1.0372e1, 1.0291e1, 1.021e1, 1.0129e1, 1.0048e1, 9.967e0, 9.886e0, 9.805e0, 9.724e0, 9.643e0, 9.562e0, 1.0481e1, mu = 1.1e2, alternative = 0e0 gives -3.4104308388405693e3; 0e0 (mpmath -3.410430838840569e3; 8.683030241479338e-253), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::stats_tests::chisquare::chisquare`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/chisquare.jsonl`: Chi-squared goodness-of-fit test: statistic sum (o - e)^2 / e over k categories and its p-value, the chi-squared survival function with k - 1 - ddof degrees of freedom
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** k in {2, 3, 5, 10, 30} categories; expected frequencies equal or proportional to 1, 2, ..., k; totals 100, 1e4, 1e6; observed counts round(e_i (1 + delta z_i)) with z_i = ((7919 i) mod 1000 - 500) / 1000 and delta in {0.001, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1} (skipped where every count equals its expectation), the expected frequencies rescaled to the counts' sum; ddof 0 and, where k > 2, 1. Statistic from 1e-3 to 2120, p-value from 0 to 1
- **Tolerance:** 5e-12 relative. For both outputs, as for ttest_1samp. The counts are exact and the statistic is a sum of k positive terms, off by up to about (kappa_s + k) 2^-53 in a careful method, kappa_s being its relative condition number in the expected frequencies (chisquare_rounding_bound); the p-value adds kappa_p = |x f(x) / Q|, about half the statistic in the tail (431 at most where the p-value is a normal double). The largest bound max(kappa_p, 1) (kappa_s + k) 2^-53 there is 4.2e-12 (k = 30 equal expected frequencies, total 1e6, delta 0.1, ddof 1: statistic 888, p 6.1e-169); 5e-12 as for ttest_1samp (tested). P-values that round to 0 (4 points) are exact in any method. SciPy's worst error is 1.9e-13
- **Worst case:** f_obs = 2.043e3, 4.481e3, 6.67e3, 8.823e3, 1.0942e4, 1.3026e4, 1.5075e4, 1.7089e4, 1.9068e4, 2.1013e4, 2.2923e4, 2.4797e4, 2.6637e4, 3.1453e4, 3.3439e4, 3.5389e4, 3.7305e4, 3.9186e4, 4.1032e4, 4.2843e4, 4.4619e4, 4.6361e4, 4.8068e4, 4.9739e4, 5.1376e4, 5.857e4, 6.0352e4, 6.21e4, 6.3812e4, 6.549e4, f_exp = 2.1585397849462365e3, 4.317079569892473e3, 6.47561935483871e3, 8.634159139784946e3, 1.0792698924731183e4, 1.295123870967742e4, 1.5109778494623655e4, 1.7268318279569892e4, 1.9426858064516127e4, 2.1585397849462366e4, 2.37439376344086e4, 2.590247741935484e4, 2.8061017204301075e4, 3.021955698924731e4, 3.237809677419355e4, 3.4536636559139784e4, 3.669517634408602e4, 3.8853716129032255e4, 4.101225591397849e4, 4.317079569892473e4, 4.532933548387097e4, 4.74878752688172e4, 4.964641505376344e4, 5.180495483870968e4, 5.396349462365591e4, 5.612203440860215e4, 5.828057419354839e4, 6.043911397849462e4, 6.259765376344086e4, 6.47561935483871e4, ddof = 1e0 gives 8.704917192005921e2; 0e0 (mpmath 8.704917192005919e2; 3.144363563052487e-165), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::stats_tests::f_oneway::f_oneway`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/f_oneway.jsonl`: One-way analysis of variance: F = (between-group mean square) / (within-group mean square) for k groups of N values and its p-value, the F survival function with k - 1 and N - k degrees of freedom; the groups are given as one sample x and their sizes
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** group sizes (2, 2), (3, 3, 3), (2, 4, 6, 8, 10), (30, 30, 30); value i (counted over all groups) of group j is loc + ((7919 i) mod 1000 - 500) / 1000 + delta j for loc in {0, 100} and delta in {0.001, 0.03, 0.3, 1, 3, 30}; x lists all values and sizes the group sizes. F from 0.33 to 3.0e5, p-value from 5.8e-168 to 0.72
- **Tolerance:** 5e-12 relative. For both outputs, as for ttest_1samp. The inputs are exact, but F is formed from sums of N rounded terms, so a careful (two-pass) method is off by up to about (kappa_F + N) 2^-53, kappa_F being F's relative condition number in the data (f_oneway_rounding_bound); the p-value adds kappa_p = |F f(F) / Q|. The largest bound max(kappa_p, 1) (kappa_F + N) 2^-53 on this grid is 4.2e-12 (three groups of 30 around 100 with delta 30: F = 3.0e5, kappa_p = 43); 5e-12 as for ttest_1samp (tested). SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 5.0e-12; it is off at 4: one statistic (5.1e-12, F = 1.2e5) and four p-values below 1e-52 (7.4e-12 to 7.5e-11)
- **Worst case:** x = 9.95e1, 1.00419e2, 1.00338e2, 1.00257e2, 1.00176e2, 1.00095e2, 1.00014e2, 9.9933e1, 9.9852e1, 9.9771e1, 9.969e1, 9.9609e1, 9.9528e1, 1.00447e2, 1.00366e2, 1.00285e2, 1.00204e2, 1.00123e2, 1.00042e2, 9.9961e1, 9.988e1, 9.9799e1, 9.9718e1, 9.9637e1, 9.9556e1, 1.00475e2, 1.00394e2, 1.00313e2, 1.00232e2, 1.00151e2, 1.3007e2, 1.29989e2, 1.2990800000000002e2, 1.29827e2, 1.2974599999999998e2, 1.2966500000000002e2, 1.29584e2, 1.29503e2, 1.30422e2, 1.30341e2, 1.3026e2, 1.30179e2, 1.30098e2, 1.30017e2, 1.29936e2, 1.2985500000000002e2, 1.29774e2, 1.2969299999999998e2, 1.29612e2, 1.29531e2, 1.3045e2, 1.30369e2, 1.30288e2, 1.30207e2, 1.30126e2, 1.3004500000000002e2, 1.29964e2, 1.2988299999999998e2, 1.2980200000000002e2, 1.29721e2, 1.5964e2, 1.59559e2, 1.60478e2, 1.60397e2, 1.60316e2, 1.60235e2, 1.60154e2, 1.6007299999999998e2, 1.5999200000000002e2, 1.59911e2, 1.5982999999999998e2, 1.59749e2, 1.59668e2, 1.59587e2, 1.59506e2, 1.60425e2, 1.60344e2, 1.60263e2, 1.6018200000000002e2, 1.60101e2, 1.6001999999999998e2, 1.59939e2, 1.59858e2, 1.59777e2, 1.59696e2, 1.59615e2, 1.59534e2, 1.60453e2, 1.60372e2, 1.60291e2, sizes = 3e1, 3e1, 3e1 gives 3.040881571310127e5; 0e0 (mpmath 3.040881571149036e5; 5.769017378955268e-168), rel. error 1.0e0

## `statrs::stats_tests::skewtest::skewtest`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/skewtest.jsonl`: D'Agostino's skewness test: z from the sample skewness and its p-value from the standard normal; alternative -1 less, 0 two-sided, 1 greater
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** samples of n in {8, 10, 20, 50, 100} values: loc + u_i + s u_i^2 with u_i = ((7919 i) mod 1000 - 500) / 1000, loc in {0, 10} and s in {-3, -0.3, 0.01, 0.3, 3}; loc + u_i plus 50 at i = 0 (an outlier); and the exactly symmetric loc + i - (n + 1) / 2, i = 1..n; each with alternative -1 (less), 0 (two-sided) and 1 (greater). |z| from 0 to 12.1, p-value from 4.3e-34 to 1
- **Tolerance:** 2e-12 relative. For both outputs, by the rule of ttest_1samp. The skewness b1 = m3 / m2^(3/2) is formed from sums of n rounded terms, so a careful method is off by up to about (kappa_z + n) 2^-53, kappa_z being z's relative condition number in the data (skewtest_rounding_bound); the p-value adds kappa_p = |z phi(z) / P|. The largest bound max(kappa_p, 1) (kappa_z + n) 2^-53 on this grid is 1.6e-12 (n = 100 with an outlier, z = 12.1); 2e-12 is the smallest round value above it (tested). For the exactly symmetric samples every operation in the moments is exact in double arithmetic, so a careful method returns z = 0 exactly (tested). SciPy's worst error is 1.5e-12 except at those symmetric samples, where it returns z = 1.01 to 1.04 instead of 0
- **Worst case:** x = 5e-1, 1.5e0, 2.5e0, 3.5e0, 4.5e0, 5.5e0, 6.5e0, 7.5e0, 8.5e0, 9.5e0, 1.05e1, 1.15e1, 1.25e1, 1.35e1, 1.45e1, 1.55e1, 1.65e1, 1.75e1, 1.85e1, 1.95e1, alternative = 1e0 gives 1.0403494689426542e0; 1.4908878450721152e-1 (mpmath 0e0; 5e-1), rel. error 4.7e307

## `statrs::distribution::Beta::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/betaincinv.jsonl`: Inverse of the regularized incomplete beta function: the x with I_x(a, b) = p (the beta distribution's quantile function)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** a, b in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 100, 1000, 10000} x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.9, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 and 1 at p = 1 (the ends of the support, so not poles); for small a the lower-tail quantiles are below the smallest double, and for small b the upper-tail quantiles round to 1
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. a, b and p are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the lower tail the quantile is x = exp(L) with L = log(p a B(a, b)) / a approximately; rounding L (or 1/a, which multiplies it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a nonzero, normal quantile below 1 on this grid is 700.0 (a = 1, b = 1e4, p = 1e-300, x = 1e-304), so up to 7.8e-14 is unavoidable; near x = 1 the same holds for 1 - x, which moves x by less. 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches t_ppf and gamma_ppf. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 2.0e-13 (a = 1000, b = 1e4, p = 0.75); it fails 105 points: NaN for 29 points with p <= 1e-300, about 2.2e-308 (just below the smallest normal double) where the quantile is smaller (41 points), errors from 1.2e-12 to 911x at 33 points with p <= 1e-300 (e.g. 0.375 instead of 0.477 at a = 1000, b = 0.5, p = 5e-324), and 2.8e-13 and 2.1e-13 at a = 1000, b = 1e4, p = 0.999 and 1 - 1e-5
- **Worst case:** a = 1e4, b = 1e4, p = 1e-300 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XOutOfRange (mpmath 3.712325170640977e-1), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::FisherSnedecor::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/f_ppf.jsonl`: F (Fisher-Snedecor) distribution quantile function F^-1(p; d1, d2)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** d1, d2 in {0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 100, 1000, 1e5} (every pair) x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.9, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; the quantile is 0 at p = 0 (the end of the support) and p = 1 is tagged pole (+inf); quantiles beyond the largest double are tagged overflow
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. d1, d2 and p are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the tails the quantile is x = exp(L) with L of the order of log(p) / a (lower tail) or -log(1 - p) / b (upper tail); rounding L (or 1/a, 1/b, d2/d1, which multiply it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a positive, normal quantile on this grid is 690.8 (d1 = 2, p = 1e-300, x = 1e-300), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches betaincinv. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.8e-13 (d1 = d2 = 1000, p = 1e-100); it fails 101 points, all with p <= 1e-100: NaN at 27 points, numbers from 1.1e-308 to 4.5e-303 where the quantile is 0 or subnormal (41 points), and errors from 8.5e-12 to 911x at 33 points (e.g. 3e-5 instead of 6.7e-4 at d1 = 1e5, d2 = 1, p = 5e-324)
- **Worst case:** d1 = 1e5, d2 = 1e5, p = 1e-300 gives panic: called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: XOutOfRange (mpmath 7.90906854867988e-1), rel. error inf

## `statrs::distribution::StudentsT::inverse_cdf`

- **Dataset:** `reference/data/t_ppf.jsonl`: Student's t quantile function F^-1(p; df) (location 0, scale 1)
- **References:** mpmath 1.4.1, SciPy 1.18.1
- **Grid:** df in {0.1, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 7.5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6, 1e8} x p in {0, 5e-324, 1e-300, 1e-100, 1e-50, 1e-20, 1e-10, 1e-5, 1e-3, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 1/2 - 1e-10, 1/2, 1/2 + 1e-10, 0.6, 0.75, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999, and the doubles nearest 1 - 1e-5, 1 - 1e-10, 1 - 1e-15, 1}; p = 0 and 1 are tagged pole (the quantile is -inf and +inf); quantiles beyond the largest double (df <= 1 with tiny p) are tagged overflow
- **Tolerance:** 2e-13 relative. p and df are exact doubles and 1 - p is exact for p >= 1/2, but in the tail the quantile is |x| = exp(L) with L = log|x| of the order of log(1/p) / df; rounding L (or 1/df, which multiplies it) to a double changes x by |L| times 2^-53. The largest |L| at a finite, normal quantile on this grid is 689.6 (df = 1, p = 1e-300, x = -3.2e299), so up to 7.7e-14 is unavoidable; 2e-13 allows 2.6x that and matches t_cdf. SciPy's worst error at the points it gets within tolerance is 1.3e-14; it fails 36 points: up to 4.3e-7 off at p = 1/2 +- 1e-10 for 1 <= df <= 20 (0 for df = 4), 5e-4 to 9e-4 off at p = 5e-324 for df >= 1000, +inf (wrong sign) at p = 5e-324 for 2.5 <= df <= 100 (also at 1e-300 for df <= 10), and quantiles capped near -1e153 for df <= 1.5
- **Worst case:** df = 1e8, p = 9.99999999999999e-1 gives no result within 5 s (mpmath 7.94144575936834e0), rel. error inf
